```
Lemma env_ok_update : forall Gamma gamma x T u,
  env_ok Gamma gamma -> LR T u -> env_ok (update Gamma x T) (update_tm gamma x u).
Proof.
  intros Gamma gamma x T u [Hlc Hty] Hu. split.
  - intros y. unfold update_tm. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply LR_lc. exact Hu.
    + apply Hlc.
  - intros y T' Hy. unfold update_tm. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
      unfold update in Hy. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy. injection Hy as Hy. subst T'. exact Hu.
    + apply Hty with (x := y). unfold update. rewrite E. exact Hy.
Qed.
```
Careful: in the first bullet, the goal is `lc_at 0 (if Nat.eqb x y then u else gamma y)`; destruct gives `lc_at 0 u` or `lc_at 0 (gamma y)` ✓.

In the second: Hy : update Gamma x T y = Some T'; after unfold update: `(if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y) = Some T'`; destruct (Nat.eqb x y):
- true: need T' = T and LR T' u: `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy` — hmm, after `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E` and E : (x =? y) = true, Hy is already rewritten to `Some T = Some T'`. So `injection Hy as ->` gives T = T' (or T' = T) then exact Hu ✓.
- false: Hy : Gamma y = Some T' → `apply Hty with (x := y); exact Hy` ✓. Hmm, careful: Hty : forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (gamma x). So `apply Hty; exact Hy` ✓.

Let me write it simply:
```
  - intros y T' Hy. unfold update_tm. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. unfold update in Hy. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy.
      injection Hy as ->. exact Hu.
    + unfold update in Hy. rewrite E in Hy. apply Hty with (x := y). exact Hy.
```

One thing to double check: LR's Fixpoint definition and the guard checker. Let me write it:

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ lc_at 0 t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\ lc_at 0 t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```
Guard: LR T1 u and LR T2 (...) where T1, T2 are subterms ✓.

Hmm, "Not convertible" — maybe the issue is that my `change` has the wrong parenthesization. `fv c1 ++ fv c2 ++ fv c3` in Coq's notation `++` is right-associative? In Coq's List, `++` is declared as right associative: `Notation "l ++ m" := (app l m) (at level 60, right associativity)`. So `fv c1 ++ fv c2 ++ fv c3` = `fv c1 ++ (fv c2 ++ fv c3)`. But the Fixpoint body `fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3` with right associativity = `fv t1 ++ (fv t2 ++ fv t3)` ✓. So my `((fv c1 ++ fv c2) ++ fv c3)` is WRONG — it should be `(fv c1 ++ (fv c2 ++ fv c3))` = just `fv c1 ++ fv c2 ++ fv c3` (right assoc) ✓.

One important detail I should double-check: `LR` Fixpoint guard condition. Let me write:

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ lc_at 0 t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\ lc_at 0 t /\
      (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Then:
```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (gamma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  lc_env gamma /\ (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (gamma x)).
```

  forall gamma, env_ok Gamma gamma -> LR T (subst gamma t).

    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).

Wait — the fundamental theorem compiled?! That's great news. Now let me write the final theorem proof.

```
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Hlr : LR T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental. split.
    - intro x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x Ty Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  rewrite subst_id in Hlr.
  exact (LR_SN T t Hlr).
Qed.
```

Let me replace the placeholder proof.

Let me summarize the proof architecture for the user:

1. **Computable tests**: `lcb` (local closure) and `valueb` (values) with correctness lemmas; `value_dec` and a computable decision for stepping (`step_dec`), plus `step_det` (determinism) — needed to distinguish "is a value / steps / is stuck" constructively.

2. **Structural lemmas**: weakening of `lc_at`, opening preserves local closure (`lc_at_open_rec`), inversion (`lc_at_open_rec_inv`), `open_rec` is the identity on locally closed terms; free-variable substitution `subst` with `subst_open_fvar` (the key one: substituting an opened body = opening the substituted body); local closure preserved by step and by typing (`has_type_lc`).

3. **Logical relation**: `LR T t` := SN ∧ lc ∧ (forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (t u)) for arrows; Bool = SN ∧ lc. The arrow clause quantifies over ALL related arguments (not just values), which makes the T_App case immediate; the cost is handled by the "stuck term" lemma.

4. **Key lemmas**: LR → SN, LR → lc, forward and backward closure under step, `LR_stuck` (a locally closed, non-value term with no reducts is in LR at every type), `LR_abs_app` (beta-expansion for abstractions: if the body applied to every related argument is in the relation, then the abstraction applied to a related argument is in the relation — proven by induction on the SN derivation of the argument, splitting on value/step/stuck), and `LR_if` (the analogous rule for conditionals).

5. **Fundamental theorem**: for every typing derivation Γ ⊢ t : T and every environment γ mapping variables of Γ into LR, `subst γ t ∈ LR T`. The abstraction case picks a fresh atom, applies the IH under the extended environment, and uses `subst_open_fvar`.

6. **Main theorem**: the identity environment is `env_ok empty`; `subst id t = t`; LR implies SN.

I'm done. Let me write the final summary.
